It’s definitely bothering me enough that I felt like I should ask for help, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble keeping things organized, or is this a relatively new issue for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s totally new, but it became a real problem about… a little over two years ago, maybe two years and a couple months. That was around when I changed schools and had to manage the business program and my accessories shop with less structure.\n\nBefore that I could kind of get by, I think, but once there were more things I had to track on my own, it started showing up more. I don’t know if that’s the kind of timeline you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay focused or pay attention in both your schoolwork and when you're working on your accessories shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do try to pay attention. But in class, or if I’m watching a video for one of my business assignments, I’ll start out listening and feeling like I’m following it.\n\nThen after a few minutes I notice my mind has gone off to, like, charm designs or order ideas for the shop, and then I realize I missed a big chunk of what was being said. It’s kind of embarrassing, because I’m not trying to ignore it. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. I mean, I try not to make it obvious, especially if I’m supposed to be sitting and listening, but it’s like my body feels kind of… buzzy? Like I’m not going anywhere, but I still feel like I need to move somehow.\n\nI’ll shift around or want to tap my foot or mess with something, and I feel bad because I don’t want it to look rude. But sitting still for too long is honestly pretty uncomfortable. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no.   },

Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has gotten in the way.

Like I missed some required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which feels really bad because I didn’t mean to just… drop the ball like that. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always have things together when I should.

I just feel like I should be able to handle it better, but I’m not really managing it well.\n\nDoctor: How much distress are you feeling because of your difficulty keeping things organized?\n\nPatient: I mean… it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything, but it does stress me out more than I want to admit. I came in because trying to keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time has just gotten really overwhelming.\n\nLike, my supplies, due dates, receipts, assignment notes—everything ends up in piles, and then I look at it and don’t know what I’m supposed to do first. So I guess the distress is… moderate? It’s definitely bothering me enough that I felt like I should ask for help, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble keeping things organized, or is this a relatively new issue for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s totally new, but it became a real problem about… a little over two years ago, maybe two years and a couple months. That was around when I changed schools and had to manage the business program and my accessories shop with less structure.\n\nBefore that I could kind of get by, I think, but once there were more things I had to track on my own, it started showing up more. I don’t know if that’s the kind of timeline you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay focused or pay attention in both your schoolwork and when you're working on your accessories shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do try to pay attention. But in class, or if I’m watching a video for one of my business assignments, I’ll start out listening and feeling like I’m following it.\n\nThen after a few minutes I notice my mind has gone off to, like, charm designs or order ideas for the shop, and then I realize I missed a big chunk of what was being said. It’s kind of embarrassing, because I’m not trying to ignore it. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. I mean, I try not to make it obvious, especially if I’m supposed to be sitting and listening, but it’s like my body feels kind of… buzzy? Like I’m not going anywhere, but I still feel like I need to move somehow.\n\nI’ll shift around or want to tap my foot or mess with something, and I feel bad because I don’t want it to look rude. But sitting still for too long is honestly pretty uncomfortable. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in your relationships with friends or family due to your organizational issues or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulties with organization and restlessness are interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nIt’s not like every single part of my life is falling apart, but it has become significant. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. I mean, I try not to make it obvious, especially if I’m supposed to be sitting and listening, but it’s like my body feels kind of… buzzy? Like I’m not going anywhere, but I still feel like I need to move somehow.\n\nI’ll shift around or want to tap my foot or mess with something, and I feel bad because I don’t want it to look rude. But sitting still for too long is honestly pretty uncomfortable. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in your relationships with friends or family due to your organizational issues or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I don’t think anyone hates me or anything like that, but it has caused some problems.\n\nWith my school program, I missed some required steps I was supposed to do, and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really stressful and embarrassing to say. And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no. It kind of shows up at home too, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ll bring my school papers or shop supplies home and mean to sort them, but then my receipts, due dates, assignment notes, little charms and packaging stuff… they all end up in piles. I tell myself I’ll make a system, but then I don’t know what to do first, so I just move things around or put it off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to handle things, because I am trying, but that’s honestly a big part of why I came in. I feel like I can’t get my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks or achieving your goals due to your organizational issues and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has gotten in the way.\n\nLike I missed some required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which feels really bad because I didn’t mean to just… drop the ball like that. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always have things together when I should.\n\nSo yes, I guess it is affecting goals, even if I’m trying to fix it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulties with organization and restlessness are interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nIt’s not like every single part of my life is falling apart, but it has become significant. I missed required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which is really stressful. It’s not only when I’m doing school or shop stuff.\n\nLike, even if I’m just supposed to be relaxing or sitting and listening to someone, I’ll feel this kind of buzzing in my body, like I should be moving or getting up even if there’s nowhere I need to go. I try not to make it obvious, because I know that can seem rude, but it’s there.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as restlessness in the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in your relationships with friends or family due to your organizational issues or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I don’t think anyone hates me or anything like that, but it has caused some problems.\n\nWith my school program, I missed some required steps I was supposed to do, and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really stressful and embarrassing to say. And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no. It kind of shows up at home too, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ll bring my school papers or shop supplies home and mean to sort them, but then my receipts, due dates, assignment notes, little charms and packaging stuff… they all end up in piles. I tell myself I’ll make a system, but then I don’t know what to do first, so I just move things around or put it off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to handle things, because I am trying, but that’s honestly a big part of why I came in. I feel like I can’t get my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks or achieving your goals due to your organizational issues and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has gotten in the way.\n\nLike I missed some required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which feels really bad because I didn’t mean to just… drop the ball like that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulties with organization and restlessness are interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nIt’s not like every single part of my life is falling apart, but it has become significant. I missed required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which is really stressful. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I haven’t always had things organized or ready.\n\nSo… yes, I think it is interfering in a real way. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness during leisure activities or when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it can happen then too. It’s not only when I’m doing school or shop stuff.\n\nLike, even if I’m just supposed to be relaxing or sitting and listening to someone, I’ll feel this kind of buzzing in my body, like I should be moving or getting up even if there’s nowhere I need to go. I try not to make it obvious, because I know that can seem rude, but it’s there.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as restlessness in the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Yeah… even with things I like, honestly. I mean, it’s not that I’m not enjoying them, so I don’t want it to sound like that. But if I’m supposed to sit still and listen, I can feel this buzzing in my body, like I need to move or shift around.

It’s kind of embarrassing, because I’m not trying to ignore it. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. I mean, I try not to make it obvious, especially if I’m supposed to be sitting and listening, but it’s like my body feels kind of… buzzy? Like I’m not going anywhere, but I still feel like I need to move somehow.\n\nI’ll shift around or want to tap my foot or mess with something, and I feel bad because I don’t want it to look rude. But sitting still for too long is honestly pretty uncomfortable. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in your relationships with friends or family due to your organizational issues or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I don’t think anyone hates me or anything like that, but it has caused some problems.\n\nWith my school program, I missed some required steps I was supposed to do, and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really stressful and embarrassing to say. And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no. It kind of shows up at home too, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ll bring my school papers or shop supplies home and mean to sort them, but then my receipts, due dates, assignment notes, little charms and packaging stuff… they all end up in piles. I tell myself I’ll make a system, but then I don’t know what to do first, so I just move things around or put it off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to handle things, because I am trying, but that’s honestly a big part of why I came in. I feel like I can’t get my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks or achieving your goals due to your organizational issues and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has gotten in the way.\n\nLike I missed some required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which feels really bad because I didn’t mean to just… drop the ball like that. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always have things together when I should.\n\nSo yes, I guess it is affecting goals, even if I’m trying to fix it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulties with organization and restlessness are interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nIt’s not like every single part of my life is falling apart, but it has become significant. I missed required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which is really stressful. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I haven’t always had things organized or ready.\n\nSo… yes, I think it is interfering in a real way. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness during leisure activities or when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it can happen then too. It’s not only when I’m doing school or shop stuff.\n\nLike, even if I’m just supposed to be relaxing or sitting and listening to someone, I’ll feel this kind of buzzing in my body, like I should be moving or getting up even if there’s nowhere I need to go. I try not to make it obvious, because I know that can seem rude, but it’s there.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as restlessness in the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t think I do anything really dangerous or anything like that, but I do catch myself jumping in too fast.\n\nLike in group critiques or class discussions, I’ll start answering before the other person is even finished talking. Then I realize I kind of guessed where they were going, and I feel embarrassed because maybe they weren’t even asking what I thought they were asking.\n\nI try not to, but it happens. Is that the kind of impulse you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience difficulties with organization and restlessness even during weekends or vacations when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s easier to notice when I’m supposed to be doing school or shop stuff, but even on weekends or when I’m technically “off,” I still get that feeling.\n\nLike if I’m just sitting with people or trying to relax and listen, my body feels kind of… buzzy, I guess. Like I need to move, even if I’m not actually going anywhere. I try to keep it subtle because I don’t want to seem rude or like I’m not paying attention.\n\nSo yes, I think it still happens then too. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty with organization even when you're doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even with things I like, honestly. I mean, it’s not that I’m not enjoying them, so I don’t want it to sound like that. But if I’m supposed to sit still and listen, I can feel this buzzing in my body, like I need to move or shift around.\n\nEven when I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body thinks I should be. I try to keep it small so it’s not annoying to other people. I’ll look at it all and not really know what I’m supposed to do first. I just feel like I should be able to handle it better, but I’m not really managing it well.\n\nDoctor: How much distress are you feeling because of your difficulty keeping things organized?\n\nPatient: I mean… it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything, but it does stress me out more than I want to admit. I came in because trying to keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time has just gotten really overwhelming.\n\nLike, my supplies, due dates, receipts, assignment notes—everything ends up in piles, and then I look at it and don’t know what I’m supposed to do first. So I guess the distress is… moderate? It’s definitely bothering me enough that I felt like I should ask for help, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble keeping things organized, or is this a relatively new issue for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s totally new, but it became a real problem about… a little over two years ago, maybe two years and a couple months. That was around when I changed schools and had to manage the business program and my accessories shop with less structure.\n\nBefore that I could kind of get by, I think, but once there were more things I had to track on my own, it started showing up more. I don’t know if that’s the kind of timeline you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay focused or pay attention in both your schoolwork and when you're working on your accessories shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do try to pay attention. But in class, or if I’m watching a video for one of my business assignments, I’ll start out listening and feeling like I’m following it.\n\nThen after a few minutes I notice my mind has gone off to, like, charm designs or order ideas for the shop, and then I realize I missed a big chunk of what was being said. It’s kind of embarrassing, because I’m not trying to ignore it. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. I mean, I try not to make it obvious, especially if I’m supposed to be sitting and listening, but it’s like my body feels kind of… buzzy? Like I’m not going anywhere, but I still feel like I need to move somehow.\n\nI’ll shift around or want to tap my foot or mess with something, and I feel bad because I don’t want it to look rude. But sitting still for too long is honestly pretty uncomfortable. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in your relationships with friends or family due to your organizational issues or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I don’t think anyone hates me or anything like that, but it has caused some problems.\n\nWith my school program, I missed some required steps I was supposed to do, and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really stressful and embarrassing to say. And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no. It kind of shows up at home too, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ll bring my school papers or shop supplies home and mean to sort them, but then my receipts, due dates, assignment notes, little charms and packaging stuff… they all end up in piles. I tell myself I’ll make a system, but then I don’t know what to do first, so I just move things around or put it off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to handle things, because I am trying, but that’s honestly a big part of why I came in. I feel like I can’t get my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks or achieving your goals due to your organizational issues and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has gotten in the way.\n\nLike I missed some required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which feels really bad because I didn’t mean to just… drop the ball like that. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always have things together when I should.\n\nSo yes, I guess it is affecting goals, even if I’m trying to fix it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulties with organization and restlessness are interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nIt’s not like every single part of my life is falling apart, but it has become significant. I missed required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which is really stressful. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I haven’t always had things organized or ready.\n\nSo… yes, I think it is interfering in a real way. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness during leisure activities or when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it can happen then too. It’s not only when I’m doing school or shop stuff.\n\nLike, even if I’m just supposed to be relaxing or sitting and listening to someone, I’ll feel this kind of buzzing in my body, like I should be moving or getting up even if there’s nowhere I need to go. I try not to make it obvious, because I know that can seem rude, but it’s there.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as restlessness in the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t think I do anything really dangerous or anything like that, but I do catch myself jumping in too fast.\n\nLike in group critiques or class discussions, I’ll start answering before the other person is even finished talking. Then I realize I kind of guessed where they were going, and I feel embarrassed because maybe they weren’t even asking what I thought they were asking.\n\nI try not to, but it happens. Is that the kind of impulse you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience difficulties with organization and restlessness even during weekends or vacations when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s easier to notice when I’m supposed to be doing school or shop stuff, but even on weekends or when I’m technically “off,” I still get that feeling.\n\nLike if I’m just sitting with people or trying to relax and listen, my body feels kind of… buzzy, I guess. Like I need to move, even if I’m not actually going anywhere. I try to keep it subtle because I don’t want to seem rude or like I’m not paying attention.\n\nSo yes, I think it still happens then too. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty with organization even when you're doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even with things I like, honestly. I mean, it’s not that I’m not enjoying them, so I don’t want it to sound like that. But if I’m supposed to sit still and listen, I can feel this buzzing in my body, like I need to move or shift around.\n\nEven when I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body thinks I should be. I try to keep it small so it’s not annoying to other people. But yes, I think it still happens with enjoyable things too.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty with organization at home with your family as well?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at home too, I think. I don’t mean to make it a thing, and I don’t think my family always notices, but if I’m supposed to just sit and listen, I get that same kind of buzzing feeling in my body.\n\nLike even if I’m not actually going anywhere or needing to do anything, I feel like I should be moving or shifting around. I try to keep it small, so it doesn’t seem like I’m being rude or impatient. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… I don’t think I’ve ever been formally diagnosed with that, no. And with my family, I’m not really sure—no one has told me they have it, at least.

I guess it’s possible there are things people just didn’t talk about, but I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know. Is that okay?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a hard time keeping things organized. I just feel like I should be able to handle it better, but I’m not really managing it well.\n\nDoctor: How much distress are you feeling because of your difficulty keeping things organized?\n\nPatient: I mean… it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything, but it does stress me out more than I want to admit. I came in because trying to keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time has just gotten really overwhelming.\n\nLike, my supplies, due dates, receipts, assignment notes—everything ends up in piles, and then I look at it and don’t know what I’m supposed to do first. So I guess the distress is… moderate? It’s definitely bothering me enough that I felt like I should ask for help, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble keeping things organized, or is this a relatively new issue for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s totally new, but it became a real problem about… a little over two years ago, maybe two years and a couple months. That was around when I changed schools and had to manage the business program and my accessories shop with less structure.\n\nBefore that I could kind of get by, I think, but once there were more things I had to track on my own, it started showing up more. I don’t know if that’s the kind of timeline you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay focused or pay attention in both your schoolwork and when you're working on your accessories shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I do try to pay attention. But in class, or if I’m watching a video for one of my business assignments, I’ll start out listening and feeling like I’m following it.\n\nThen after a few minutes I notice my mind has gone off to, like, charm designs or order ideas for the shop, and then I realize I missed a big chunk of what was being said. It’s kind of embarrassing, because I’m not trying to ignore it. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. I mean, I try not to make it obvious, especially if I’m supposed to be sitting and listening, but it’s like my body feels kind of… buzzy? Like I’m not going anywhere, but I still feel like I need to move somehow.\n\nI’ll shift around or want to tap my foot or mess with something, and I feel bad because I don’t want it to look rude. But sitting still for too long is honestly pretty uncomfortable. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in your relationships with friends or family due to your organizational issues or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I don’t think anyone hates me or anything like that, but it has caused some problems.\n\nWith my school program, I missed some required steps I was supposed to do, and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really stressful and embarrassing to say. And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no. It kind of shows up at home too, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ll bring my school papers or shop supplies home and mean to sort them, but then my receipts, due dates, assignment notes, little charms and packaging stuff… they all end up in piles. I tell myself I’ll make a system, but then I don’t know what to do first, so I just move things around or put it off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to handle things, because I am trying, but that’s honestly a big part of why I came in. I feel like I can’t get my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks or achieving your goals due to your organizational issues and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has gotten in the way.\n\nLike I missed some required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which feels really bad because I didn’t mean to just… drop the ball like that. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always have things together when I should.\n\nSo yes, I guess it is affecting goals, even if I’m trying to fix it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulties with organization and restlessness are interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nIt’s not like every single part of my life is falling apart, but it has become significant. I missed required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which is really stressful. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I haven’t always had things organized or ready.\n\nSo… yes, I think it is interfering in a real way. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness during leisure activities or when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it can happen then too. It’s not only when I’m doing school or shop stuff.\n\nLike, even if I’m just supposed to be relaxing or sitting and listening to someone, I’ll feel this kind of buzzing in my body, like I should be moving or getting up even if there’s nowhere I need to go. I try not to make it obvious, because I know that can seem rude, but it’s there.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as restlessness in the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t think I do anything really dangerous or anything like that, but I do catch myself jumping in too fast.\n\nLike in group critiques or class discussions, I’ll start answering before the other person is even finished talking. Is that the kind of impulse you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience difficulties with organization and restlessness even during weekends or vacations when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like I need to move, even if I’m not actually going anywhere. I try to keep it subtle because I don’t want to seem rude or like I’m not paying attention.\n\nSo yes, I think it still happens then too. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty with organization even when you're doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even with things I like, honestly. I mean, it’s not that I’m not enjoying them, so I don’t want it to sound like that. But if I’m supposed to sit still and listen, I can feel this buzzing in my body, like I need to move or shift around.\n\nEven when I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body thinks I should be. I try to keep it small so it’s not annoying to other people. I try to keep it small, so it doesn’t seem like I’m being rude or impatient. Yeah… I mean, I try not to, but I do have trouble keeping track of what I’m supposed to do.

I came in because trying to keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time has just gotten really overwhelming.\n\nLike, my supplies, due dates, receipts, assignment notes—everything ends up in piles, and then I look at it and don’t know what I’m supposed to do first. So I guess the distress is… moderate? It’s definitely bothering me enough that I felt like I should ask for help, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble keeping things organized, or is this a relatively new issue for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s totally new, but it became a real problem about… a little over two years ago, maybe two years and a couple months. It’s kind of embarrassing, because I’m not trying to ignore it. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. I mean, I try not to make it obvious, especially if I’m supposed to be sitting and listening, but it’s like my body feels kind of… buzzy? Like I’m not going anywhere, but I still feel like I need to move somehow.\n\nI’ll shift around or want to tap my foot or mess with something, and I feel bad because I don’t want it to look rude. But sitting still for too long is honestly pretty uncomfortable. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in your relationships with friends or family due to your organizational issues or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I don’t think anyone hates me or anything like that, but it has caused some problems.\n\nWith my school program, I missed some required steps I was supposed to do, and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really stressful and embarrassing to say. And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no. It kind of shows up at home too, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ll bring my school papers or shop supplies home and mean to sort them, but then my receipts, due dates, assignment notes, little charms and packaging stuff… they all end up in piles. I feel like I can’t get my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks or achieving your goals due to your organizational issues and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has gotten in the way.\n\nLike I missed some required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which feels really bad because I didn’t mean to just… drop the ball like that. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always have things together when I should.\n\nSo yes, I guess it is affecting goals, even if I’m trying to fix it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulties with organization and restlessness are interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nIt’s not like every single part of my life is falling apart, but it has become significant. I missed required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which is really stressful. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I haven’t always had things organized or ready.\n\nSo… yes, I think it is interfering in a real way. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness during leisure activities or when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it can happen then too. It’s not only when I’m doing school or shop stuff.\n\nLike, even if I’m just supposed to be relaxing or sitting and listening to someone, I’ll feel this kind of buzzing in my body, like I should be moving or getting up even if there’s nowhere I need to go. I try not to make it obvious, because I know that can seem rude, but it’s there.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as restlessness in the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t think I do anything really dangerous or anything like that, but I do catch myself jumping in too fast.\n\nLike in group critiques or class discussions, I’ll start answering before the other person is even finished talking. Then I realize I kind of guessed where they were going, and I feel embarrassed because maybe they weren’t even asking what I thought they were asking.\n\nI try not to, but it happens. Is that the kind of impulse you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience difficulties with organization and restlessness even during weekends or vacations when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty with organization even when you're doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even with things I like, honestly. I try to keep it small so it’s not annoying to other people. And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no. I missed required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which is really stressful. It’s not only when I’m doing school or shop stuff.\n\nLike, even if I’m just supposed to be relaxing or sitting and listening to someone, I’ll feel this kind of buzzing in my body, like I should be moving or getting up even if there’s nowhere I need to go. I try not to make it obvious, because I know that can seem rude, but it’s there.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as restlessness in the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Then I realize I kind of guessed where they were going, and I feel embarrassed because maybe they weren’t even asking what I thought they were asking.\n\nI try not to, but it happens. Is that the kind of impulse you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience difficulties with organization and restlessness even during weekends or vacations when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty with organization even when you're doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even with things I like, honestly. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always have things together when I should.\n\nSo yes, I guess it is affecting goals, even if I’m trying to fix it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulties with organization and restlessness are interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nIt’s not like every single part of my life is falling apart, but it has become significant. I missed required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which is really stressful. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I haven’t always had things organized or ready.\n\nSo… yes, I think it is interfering in a real way. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness during leisure activities or when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it can happen then too. It’s not only when I’m doing school or shop stuff.\n\nLike, even if I’m just supposed to be relaxing or sitting and listening to someone, I’ll feel this kind of buzzing in my body, like I should be moving or getting up even if there’s nowhere I need to go. I try not to make it obvious, because I know that can seem rude, but it’s there.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as restlessness in the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often interrupt others when they're speaking?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 21 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Blurting_Answers\nmanifestation: \"In group critiques or class discussions, I jump in with my answer before the other person is done talking, and then I feel embarrassed because I realize I guessed what they were going to say.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Sometimes, yeah… I don’t mean to be rude, and I try to catch myself, but in class discussions or group critiques I’ll jump in with an answer before the other person is fully done talking.

Then I realize I kind of guessed where they were going, and sometimes I’m wrong, or I just feel embarrassed because I interrupted. I usually feel bad right after. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks for seeing me. I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a hard time keeping things organized. It’s probably not a huge thing, but it’s starting to feel like too much.\n\nI’m trying to manage my schoolwork and also this little accessories shop I have, and everything kind of ends up in piles—supplies, receipts, assignment notes, due dates. I’ll look at it all and not really know what I’m supposed to do first. I just feel like I should be able to handle it better, but I’m not really managing it well.\n\nDoctor: How much distress are you feeling because of your difficulty keeping things organized?\n\nPatient: I mean… it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything, but it does stress me out more than I want to admit. I came in because trying to keep my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time has just gotten really overwhelming.\n\nLike, my supplies, due dates, receipts, assignment notes—everything ends up in piles, and then I look at it and don’t know what I’m supposed to do first. So I guess the distress is… moderate? It’s definitely bothering me enough that I felt like I should ask for help, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble keeping things organized, or is this a relatively new issue for you?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say it’s totally new, but it became a real problem about… a little over two years ago, maybe two years and a couple months. That was around when I changed schools and had to manage the business program and my accessories shop with less structure.\n\nBefore that I could kind of get by, I think, but once there were more things I had to track on my own, it started showing up more. I don’t know if that’s the kind of timeline you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay focused or pay attention in both your schoolwork and when you're working on your accessories shop?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. But in class, or if I’m watching a video for one of my business assignments, I’ll start out listening and feeling like I’m following it.\n\nThen after a few minutes I notice my mind has gone off to, like, charm designs or order ideas for the shop, and then I realize I missed a big chunk of what was being said. It’s kind of embarrassing, because I’m not trying to ignore it. Is that what you mean by trouble focusing?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. I mean, I try not to make it obvious, especially if I’m supposed to be sitting and listening, but it’s like my body feels kind of… buzzy? Like I’m not going anywhere, but I still feel like I need to move somehow.\n\nI’ll shift around or want to tap my foot or mess with something, and I feel bad because I don’t want it to look rude. But sitting still for too long is honestly pretty uncomfortable. Is that the kind of restlessness you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulties in your relationships with friends or family due to your organizational issues or restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a little. I mean, I don’t think anyone hates me or anything like that, but it has caused some problems.\n\nWith my school program, I missed some required steps I was supposed to do, and now my financial aid is kind of at risk, which is… really stressful and embarrassing to say. And with the shop stuff, people I work with on projects have started relying on me less, I think, because I don’t always have things organized or ready when I’m supposed to.\n\nI don’t mean to make excuses. I’m trying, but it has affected things more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness at home as well, or are they mainly happening at school or with your shop?\n\nPatient: It’s not only school or the shop, no. It kind of shows up at home too, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ll bring my school papers or shop supplies home and mean to sort them, but then my receipts, due dates, assignment notes, little charms and packaging stuff… they all end up in piles. I tell myself I’ll make a system, but then I don’t know what to do first, so I just move things around or put it off.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m completely unable to handle things, because I am trying, but that’s honestly a big part of why I came in. I feel like I can’t get my schoolwork and my little accessories shop organized at the same time. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble completing tasks or achieving your goals due to your organizational issues and restlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it has gotten in the way.\n\nLike I missed some required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which feels really bad because I didn’t mean to just… drop the ball like that. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I don’t always have things together when I should.\n\nSo yes, I guess it is affecting goals, even if I’m trying to fix it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your difficulties with organization and restlessness are interfering with your daily life in a significant way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I’d have to say yes, even though I don’t like admitting that.\n\nIt’s not like every single part of my life is falling apart, but it has become significant. I missed required steps for my program, and now my financial aid is at risk, which is really stressful. And with the shop projects, people have started depending on me less because I haven’t always had things organized or ready.\n\nSo… yes, I think it is interfering in a real way. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these difficulties with organization and restlessness during leisure activities or when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it can happen then too. It’s not only when I’m doing school or shop stuff.\n\nLike, even if I’m just supposed to be relaxing or sitting and listening to someone, I’ll feel this kind of buzzing in my body, like I should be moving or getting up even if there’s nowhere I need to go. I try not to make it obvious, because I know that can seem rude, but it’s there.\n\nI’m not sure if that counts as restlessness in the way you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking things through?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes. I don’t think I do anything really dangerous or anything like that, but I do catch myself jumping in too fast.\n\nLike in group critiques or class discussions, I’ll start answering before the other person is even finished talking. Then I realize I kind of guessed where they were going, and I feel embarrassed because maybe they weren’t even asking what I thought they were asking.\n\nI try not to, but it happens. Is that the kind of impulse you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience difficulties with organization and restlessness even during weekends or vacations when you're not working on your shop or schoolwork?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s easier to notice when I’m supposed to be doing school or shop stuff, but even on weekends or when I’m technically “off,” I still get that feeling.\n\nLike if I’m just sitting with people or trying to relax and listen, my body feels kind of… buzzy, I guess. Like I need to move, even if I’m not actually going anywhere. I try to keep it subtle because I don’t want to seem rude or like I’m not paying attention.\n\nSo yes, I think it still happens then too. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty with organization even when you're doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… even with things I like, honestly. I mean, it’s not that I’m not enjoying them, so I don’t want it to sound like that. But if I’m supposed to sit still and listen, I can feel this buzzing in my body, like I need to move or shift around.\n\nEven when I’m not actually going anywhere, it feels like my body thinks I should be. I try to keep it small so it’s not annoying to other people. But yes, I think it still happens with enjoyable things too.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty with organization at home with your family as well?\n\nPatient: Yeah… at home too, I think. I try to keep it small, so it doesn’t seem like I’m being rude or impatient. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been diagnosed with attention deficit hyperactivity disorder or does anyone in your family have it?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve ever been formally diagnosed with that, no. And with my family, I’m not really sure—no one has told me they have it, at least.\n\nI guess it’s possible there are things people just didn’t talk about, but I don’t want to say yes if I don’t actually know. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these feelings of restlessness and difficulty with organization consistently, regardless of the time of day or the activity you're doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think it’s pretty consistent. I try not to make it obvious, though. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often forget things you're supposed to do or have trouble remembering appointments and deadlines?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I try not to, but I do have trouble keeping track of what I’m supposed to do.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, actually.
